We answer three s<sub>y</sub>stems <sub>q</sub>uestions. (Q1) How can a full CFR iteration be com<sub>p</sub>iled into fixed tensor dataflow while <sub>p</sub>reservin<sub>g</sub> its u<sub>p</sub>date semantics? 1. A game-to-dataflow compiler for tabular CFR. 2. CUDA Graph Replay with a causal performance decomposition. 3. State-of-the-art GPU performance for tabular CFR. GPU-CFR runs 29.8–80.4× faster than the matched A100 baseline across the ei<sub>g</sub>ht-<sub>g</sub>ame suite and out<sub>p</sub>erforms ever<sub>y</sub> tested CPU <sub>p</sub>ath (Section 7).

## 2. Related Work

The CFR family and exact equilibrium solving. CFR (Zinkevich et al., 2007) minimizes counterfactual re<sub>g</sub>ret inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub> at ever<sub>y</sub> information set. In two-<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er zero-sum <sub>g</sub>ames the avera<sub>g</sub>e strate<sub>gy p</sub>rofile conver<sub>g</sub>es to a Nash e<sub>q</sub>uilibrium (Nash Jr, 1950). CFR<sup>+</sup> (Tammelin, 2014) re<sub>p</sub>laces re<sub>g</sub>ret matchin<sub>g</sub> (Hart & Mas-Colell, 2000) with re<sub>g</sub>ret matchin<sub>g</sub><sup>+</sup> and linear avera<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub>, and was the en<sub>g</sub>ine behind the essential solution of heads-u<sub>p</sub> limit hold’em (Tammelin et al., 2015; Bowlin<sub>g</sub> et al., 2015). Subse<sub>q</sub>uent variants tune the u<sub>p</sub>date rule: discountin<sub>g</sub> (Brown & Sandholm, 2019a), d<sub>y</sub>namic discountin<sub>g</sub> (Xu et al., 2024a), <sub>p</sub>redictive/o<sub>p</sub>timistic u<sub>p</sub>dates connectin<sub>g</sub> CFR to Blackwell a<sub>pp</sub>roachabilit<sub>y</sub> (Farina et al., 2021, 2019b,a; Xu et al., 2024b), and refinements of re<sub>g</sub>ret matchin<sub>g</sub> itself (Farina et al., 2023; Men<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). First-order methods reach e<sub>q</sub>uilibria b<sub>y</sub> a diferent route, smoothin<sub>g</sub> (Hoda et al., 2010; Nesterov, 2005) on the se<sub>q</sub>uence form (Koller & Pfefer, 1997). Burch et al. (Burch et al., 2019) analyze whether the two players are updated simultaneously or alternately, which changes iterate quality at matched iteration counts. Our work targets the execution layer shared b the tabular CFR famil . The com iled slot re resentation su orts vanilla CFR, CFR<sup>+</sup>, DCFR (Brown & Sandholm, 2019a), and <sub>p</sub>redictive CFR<sup>+</sup> (Farina et al., 2021) under both u<sub>p</sub>date orders.

Scaling CFR by approximation. When the full tree is too large, the community samples it, shrinks it, prunes it, or learns it. Monte Carlo CFR estimates counterfactual values from sampled trajectories (Lanctot et al., 2009), with <sub>p</sub>ublic-chance and other variance-controlled variants for solvin<sub>g</sub> and evaluation (Johanson et al., 2012; Burch et al., 2012; Schmid et al., 2019; Li et al., 2026b,a). Abstraction ma<sub>p</sub>s the <sub>g</sub>ame onto a smaller <sub>p</sub>rox<sub>y</sub> (Gil<sub>p</sub>in & Sandholm, 2007; Wau<sub>g</sub>h et al., 2009; Kroer & Sandholm, 2018; Li et al., 2024; Li & Huan<sub>g</sub>, 2025, 2026c,a), a line of work motivated directl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>y</sub> CFR’s <sub>p</sub>er-iteration cost (Johanson et al., 2012; Brown et al., 2015). Re<sub>g</sub>ret-based and online <sub>p</sub>runin<sub>g</sub> ski<sub>p</sub> low-value branches durin<sub>g</sub> the traversal (Brown & Sandholm, 2015; Li & Huan<sub>g</sub>, 2025). Dee<sub>p</sub> CFR and its successors re<sub>p</sub>lace tabular re<sub>g</sub>rets with neural a<sub>pp</sub>roximation (Brown et al., 2019; Steinber<sub>g</sub>er, 2019a), and search-based and lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-model a<sub>g</sub>ents combine learned models with online <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> (Brown et al., 2020; Schmid et al., 2023; Li et al., 2026c; Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). These methods reduce the <sub>g</sub>ame or the work sam<sub>p</sub>led from it. Our com<sub>p</sub>iler addresses the re<sub>g</sub>ime i<sub>n w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll t<sub>ree</sub> i<sub>s ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>re ea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>dl <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ver one o</sub>f th<sub>ese me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s s</sub>till <sub>runs a</sub> t<sub>a</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> CFR <sub>sweep on w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ever</sub> t<sub>ree rema</sub>i<sub>ns, so a</sub> f<sub>as</sub>t<sub>er sweep composes w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>em.</sub>

Systems and frameworks for game solving. OpenSpiel (Lanctot et al., 2020) is the field’s reference library. Its CFR im lementations favor readabilit and breadth of ame su ort. LiteEFG (Liu et al., 2024) com iles <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>ve-</sub>f<sub>orm games</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a grap</sub>h <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>th<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d $\mathrm { C } + +$ b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>en</sub>d<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e use</sub> it as the <sub>p</sub>rimar<sub>y</sub> com<sub>p</sub>iled CPU baseline. PokerRL (Steinber<sub>g</sub>er, 2019b) vectorizes CFR over hand ran<sub>g</sub>es (Johanson et al., 2012; Moravčík et al., 2017), a HUNL-s<sub>p</sub>ecific re<sub>p</sub>resentation. Its released Libratus end<sub>g</sub>ames are our external <sub>p</sub>oker reference in Section 13. Accelerated best-res<sub>p</sub>onse com<sub>p</sub>utation (Johanson et al., 2011) speeds up the evaluation half of the loop with game-specific structure. Our exploitability evaluator i<sub>s a gener</sub>i<sub>c ana</sub>l<sub>ogue</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>runs on</sub> th<sub>e same comp</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d <sub>arrays as</sub> th<sub>e so</sub>l<sub>ver.</sub> P<sub>ara</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>l CFR h<sub>as</sub> b<sub>een exp</sub>l<sub>ore</sub>d throu<sub>g</sub>h distributed sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> and e<sub>q</sub>uilibrium com<sub>p</sub>utation (Jackson, 2013; Brown et al., 2015), on CPU clusters (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2021), and in real-time solvers that <sub>p</sub>artition the traversal across CPU threads under <sub>p</sub>er-decision bud<sub>g</sub>ets (Li & Huan<sub>g</sub>, 2026e). The milestone <sub>p</sub>oker s<sub>y</sub>stems used su<sub>p</sub>ercom<sub>p</sub>uter-scale CPU parallelism (Bowling et al., 2015; Brown & Sandholm, 2018). Those designs parallelize the traversal. Accelerators already drive game simulation at scale (Koyamada et al., 2023). GPU CFR has recent precedent (Kim & Sandholm, 2026; Ba<sub>g</sub>hal, 2025), and the GPU <sub>p</sub>ath of the fastest such s<sub>y</sub>stem still runs slower than <sub>an op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d CPU <sub>so</sub>l<sub>ver.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>comp</sub>il<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e game</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>-s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>tifi<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ense</sub> t<sub>ensors an</sub>d <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> th<sub>e</sub> whole iteration as one ca<sub>p</sub>tured CUDA <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h. (a) Static chance folding. Chance behavior is strategy-independent, so for every node ℎ the product of <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ance pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es on</sub> th<sub>e roo</sub>t<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>ℎ <sub>pa</sub>th<sub>,</sub> $\pi _ { c } ( h )$ , is a build-time constant. from which the backward pass starts every iteration. The invariant is that chance probabilities appear only throu<sub>g</sub>h E<sub>q</sub>. (6). (b) Depth-level execution blocks. The forward pass must respect topology, since a child’s reach depends on its parent’s. In a tree, every child sits exactly one depth level below its parent, so grouping edges by parent depth yields a valid schedule at the coarsest granularity a depth-synchronous schedule allows. All edges at one d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>e as a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>th<sub>er, mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>y, an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>tt<sub>er, an</sub>d th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>sequen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps equa</sub>l<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> tree de<sub>p</sub>th. Across our suite this is 4–15 blocks <sub>p</sub>er <sub>p</sub>ass (Table 2). A <sub>g</sub>eneric to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>g</sub>ical schedule over (sta<sub>g</sub>e × de<sub>p</sub>th) intersections <sub>p</sub>roduced u<sub>p</sub> to 100 blocks on the same trees. The backward <sub>p</sub>ass uses the same blocks in reverse w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> index\_add scatters, w<sup>hi</sup>c<sup>h</sup> to<sup>l</sup>erate repeate<sup>d</sup> parents w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>hi</sup>n a <sup>bl</sup>oc<sup>k</sup>.

(c) Sentinel slot and dual-lane reach bufer. Player �’s reach lane $\pi _ { i }$ <sub>mus</sub>t <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>y</sub> �’<sub>s</sub> <sub>own</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>egy</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>es</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>copy</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>every</sub>thi<sub>ng e</sub>l<sub>se:</sub>

$$
\pi _ { i } ( h ^ { \prime } ) = \left\{ \begin{array} { l l } { { \pi _ { i } ( h ) \sigma ( \mathrm { s l o t } ( h , a ) ) } } & { { ( h , a , h ^ { \prime } ) \mathrm { o w n e d b y } i , } } \\ { { \pi _ { i } ( h ) } } & { { \mathrm { o t h e r w i s e } . } } \end{array} \right.\tag{7}
$$

We implement Eq. (7) branchlessly. The strategy vector is extended by one sentinel slot pinned to 1.0. For each ed<sub>g</sub>e and each lane, a <sub>p</sub>recom<sub>p</sub>uted index <sub>p</sub>oints either at the ed<sub>g</sub>e’s true slot (own-<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er ed<sub>g</sub>es) or at the sentinel (all others). The reach bufer holds both lanes conti<sub>g</sub>uousl<sub>y</sub> (2� entries), and <sub>p</sub>er-block index <sub>arrays over</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ou</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d <sub>range a</sub>d<sub>vance</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th l<sub>anes w</sub>ith <sub>a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e ga</sub>th<sub>er, mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>y, an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>tt<sub>er.</sub> Th<sub>e same</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>k removes the last per-edge branch from regret accumulation. For a player-� edge (ℎ, �) with slot $q = { \mathsf { s l o t } } ( h , a )$ th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t<sub>aneous regre</sub>t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$

Proposition 1 (Folded chance is exact). Initialize $\nu \gets \nu _ { \mathrm { t m p l } }$ as in $E q .$ . (6) and apply B<sub>ℓ</sub> for $\ell \in \mathcal { D }$ in decreasing order, with every chance edge assigned multiplier 1 through $q _ { \mathrm { v a l } }$ . $$
\nu ( h ) \ = \ \sum _ { z \mathop { \leq } h , \ z \in \mathcal { Z } } \pi _ { c } ( h \to z ) \pi _ { c } ( h ) \pi _ { 1 , 2 } ^ { \sigma } ( h \to z ) u ( z ) ,\tag{14}
$$

that $i s ,$ the chance-weighted continuation value at ℎ. In particular at the root � equals the expected payof under $\sigma ,$ and each terminal’s chance product $\pi _ { c } ( z )$ enters exactly once.

Proposition 2 (The dual-lane update is exact and branch-free). For every lane �, block ℓ, and edge $e \in B _ { \ell } ,$ , the operator $\mathrm { F } _ { \ell , i } o f E q$ . (11) evaluated with the compiled slot map Eq. (10) and $y = \sigma _ { \mathrm { e x t } }$ satisfies the case definition of Eq. (7) at �(�). The slot map is resolved at build time, so the per-iteration cost of the case distinction is zero and no per-edge predicate is evaluated during execution.

Proposition 3 (The depth schedule is shortest). Call a schedule dependency-respecting if it partitions the edges into ordered groups such that no group contains two edges $\boldsymbol { e } , \boldsymbol { e } ^ { \prime }$ with $c ( e ) = p ( e ^ { \prime } )$ , and � precedes $e ^ { \prime }$ whenever $c ( e ) = p ( e ^ { \prime } )$ . Then:

(i) the partition Eq. (9) is dependency-respecting and has � groups;

(ii) every dependency-respecting schedule has at least � groups;

(iii) the number of operator invocations per iteration is $c _ { 1 } + c _ { 2 } D$ for constants $c _ { 1 } , c _ { 2 }$ independent of � and �.

Part (iii) is the reason iteration time is nearl<sub>y</sub> flat in tree size across four decades (Table 3). Growth enters the batch widths, which the device absorbs in parallel, while the number of dispatches stays fixed. Measured on th<sub>e su</sub>it<sub>e,</sub> $c _ { 1 } \approx 3 1$ (the itemized dis<sub>p</sub>atches of Table 1 <sub>p</sub>lus framework bookkee<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub>) and $c _ { 2 } = 8$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d schedule a<sub>pp</sub>lies R once, flat over all decision ed<sub>g</sub>es (Section 4.2), so onl<sub>y</sub> F and B contribute to $c _ { 2 } .$ <sub>, a</sub>t f<sub>our</sub> <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ons per</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>eac</sub>h<sub>.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 2 <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>rms</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t � <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ree</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>on every game.</sub> Th<sub>e re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> im<sub>p</sub>lementation’s <sub>p</sub>er-(sta<sub>g</sub>e, de<sub>p</sub>th) <sub>p</sub>artition needs u<sub>p</sub> to 100 <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>s where E<sub>q</sub>. The averaging horizon. Linear averaging (Tammelin, 2014) accumulates $\bar { s } + = t \pi _ { \mathrm { o w n } }$ <sub>� on</sub> it<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> � <sub>an</sub>d <sub>never resca</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> Th<sub>e accumu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>or</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore grows qua</sub>d<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y w</sub>hil<sub>e eac</sub>h <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>t<sub>ays</sub> li<sub>near, an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo ra</sub>t<sub>es co</sub>llid<sub>e.</sub>

Proposition 4 (Representability horizon of unrescaled linear averaging). Let $\textstyle S _ { T } = \sum _ { t = 1 } ^ { T } t x _ { t }$ be the ideal real sum, with $x _ { t } \in [ 0 , 1 ] , S _ { T } = \Theta ( T ^ { 2 } )$ , and $x _ { T } \geq c > 0$ . For a binaryformat with �-bit significand, while $S _ { T }$ is in the normal range,

$$
{ \frac { T x _ { T } } { \mathrm { u l p } ( S _ { T } ) } } = \Theta \bigg ( { \frac { 2 ^ { p } } { T } } \bigg ) .\tag{15}
$$

Here ulp is the spacing in the binade containing the ideal sum (the actual rounded accumulator may sit in a neighboring binade). Without $x _ { T } \geq c ,$ only the $O ( 2 ^ { p } / T )$ boundfollows. Thus $2 ^ { p }$ is an order-of-magnitude resolution scale and should be read as such; the exact stalling point depends on the rounded trajectory. A nonnegative increment is locally absorbed under round-to-nearest if it is strictly below half the upward spacing at the actual old accumulator; ties depend on the rounding rule.

## 6. Experimental Setup

Three evaluation suites. Each suite answers a diferent experimental question. The eight-game main timing/regression suite in Table 2 contains six public games via OpenSpiel (Lanctot et al., 2020) and two native HUNL-st<sub>y</sub>le sub<sub>g</sub>ames (Ganzfried & Sandholm, 2015; Brown & Sandholm, 2017) (Section 9). The suite s<sub>p</sub>ans 54 to 275<sub>,</sub>983 Infosets and su<sub>pp</sub>orts the headline timin<sub>g,</sub> o<sub>p</sub>eration-count<sub>,</sub> and reference-<sub>p</sub>arit<sub>y</sub> results. The twelve-game update-rule suite adds four more OpenSpiel games for the four-rule, two-order CPU study of Table 6. The extended poker suite contains the released Libratus river endgames (Brown & Sandholm, 2018; Steinber<sub>g</sub>er, 2019b) and lar<sub>g</sub>er ex<sub>p</sub>orted <sub>p</sub>oker instances of the head-to-head in Section 13.

Backends. GPU-CFR runs the compiled float32 solver on one NVIDIA A100 80GB in eager or CUDA Graph <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo mo</sub>d<sub>es</sub> dif<sub>er on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub> di<sub>spa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>a van</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> CFR <sub>arm</sub> i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e eng</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> f<sub>rom regre</sub>t <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ng</sub><sup>+</sup><sub>.</sub> Ours CPU runs the same compiled tensor dataflow on eight threads pinned to dedicated physical cores, with parallelism supplied by PyTorch’s intra-operator pool (Paszke et al., 2019). LiteEFG 0.1.5 (Liu et al., 2024) uses <sub>a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>th<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d C++ b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>en</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x uses</sub> it<sub>s s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>lt<sub>aneous van</sub>ill<sub>a</sub> CFR <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e, an</sub>d S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 12 reports its alternating CFR<sup>+</sup> preset. Kim (2026) (Kim & Sandholm, 2026), released as noregret, executes se<sub>q</sub>uence-form (Koller & Pfefer, 1997) CFR throu<sub>g</sub>h CuP<sub>y</sub> s<sub>p</sub>arse <sub>p</sub>roducts on the same A100. The main matrix matches its update order to GPU-CFR’s simultaneous updates and reports its GPU backend. OpenSpiel (Lanctot et al., 2020) contributes its P<sub>y</sub>thon CFR im<sub>p</sub>lementation and exact best res<sub>p</sub>onse. Ever<sub>y</sub> backend reports exploitability as NashConv/2. Table 8 lists the rule, update order, averaging, precision, and execution mo<sup>d</sup>e <sub>p</sub>er arm.

Where the speed comes from. Update rule and update order. The rules difer only in how the two accumulators evolve. CFR<sup>+</sup> (Tammelin, 2014) clam<sub>p</sub>s accumulated re<sub>g</sub>rets at zero after each u<sub>p</sub>date and wei<sub>g</sub>hts the strate<sub>gy</sub> avera<sub>g</sub>e linearl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>y</sub> iteration index. DCFR (Brown & Sandholm, 2019a) instead discounts the accumulators: <sub>p</sub>ositive and ne<sub>g</sub>ative <sub>regre</sub>t<sub>s</sub> d<sub>ecay a</sub>t <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>exe</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es, an</sub>d th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>egy average</sub> d<sub>ecays po</sub>l<sub>ynom</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y.</sub> P<sub>re</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> CFR<sup>+</sup> (PCFR<sup>+</sup>) (Farina et al., 2021) kee<sub>p</sub>s the CFR<sup>+</sup> re<sub>g</sub>ret u<sub>p</sub>date but matches the strate<sub>gy</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ainst the accumulated regret plus the current instantaneous regret as a predictor. Under simultaneous updates both players’ regrets are computed from one strategy profile; under alternating updates the first player’s update is visible to the second <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er’s u<sub>p</sub>date in the same swee<sub>p</sub> (Burch et al., 2019).

## 9. HUNL Subgame Construction

The two native subgames. The native river subgame (3,000 Infosets) deals non-conflicting hand pairs over a fixed board and plays one street of check/bet/fold/call with exact hand evaluation. The turn subgame (83,040 Infosets) adds a 44-card river chance la<sub>y</sub>er between two bettin<sub>g</sub> streets, with �=12 hands <sub>p</sub>er <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er, sunk-cost <sub>payo</sub>f<sub>s,</sub> f<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>po</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>t <sub>s</sub>i<sub>z</sub>i<sub>ng, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>ll<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub> h<sub>an</sub>dli<sub>ng.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>oar</sub>d<sub>,</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d<sub>-range</sub> t<sub>rees cap</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> th<sub>e</sub> re<sub>p</sub>eated re-solvin<sub>g</sub> structure of HUNL-st<sub>y</sub>le workloads (Burch et al., 2014; Ganzfried & Sandholm, 2015).

Background: subgame solving and online re-solving. Decom osition solves sub ames inde endentl <sub>g</sub>iven summaries of the rest of the <sub>g</sub>ame (Burch et al., 2014), with safet<sub>y</sub> refinements (Brown & Sandholm, 2017; Zhan<sub>g</sub> & Sandholm, 2021), <sub>p</sub>ractical end<sub>g</sub>ame solvin<sub>g</sub> (Ganzfried & Sandholm, 2015; Moravcik et al., 2016), and de<sub>p</sub>th-limited variants that ca<sub>p</sub> the lookahead with value estimates (Brown et al., 2018; Kovařík et al., 2023; Kroer & Sandholm, 2020). Dee<sub>p</sub>Stack’s continual re-solvin<sub>g</sub> (Moravčík et al., 2017; <sup>Š</sup>ustr et al., 2019, 2021) and Libratus’s end<sub>g</sub>ame solver (Brown & Sandholm, 2018) run exactl<sub>y</sub> this workload online, <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er secon</sub>d<sub>-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> ti<sub>me</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> O<sub>ur</sub> t<sub>wo na</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> HUNL <sub>su</sub>b<sub>games repro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure o</sub>f thi<sub>s wor</sub>kl<sub>oa</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 7 <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r reusa</sub>bl<sub>e comp</sub>il<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on cos</sub>t f<sub>rom marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>so</sub>l<sub>ver</sub> ti<sub>me.</sub>

B<sub>o</sub>th <sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ve su</sub>b<sub>games use exac</sub>t 7<sub>-car</sub>d h<sub>an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on por</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom a pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on po</sub>k<sub>er eng</sub>i<sub>ne an</sub>d <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t b<sub>ru</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>f<sub>orce enumera</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

River subgame. Fixed board (default Ks Js Th 7d 2c), pot 20, stacks 100. A chance root deals each <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er one of �=100 candidate hands, uniforml<sub>y</sub> over non-conflictin<sub>g p</sub>airs (9,161 deals). One bettin<sub>g</sub> street: check/bet/fold/call with bet fractions {0.5, 1.0} of the pot, no raises. Payofs at showdown are ±(half pot + matched bets); folds forfeit the half-pot plus any called amount. The default instance has 3,000 Infosets on 137<sub>,</sub>415 States.

Turn subgame. Board Ks Js Th 7d plus a full 44-card river chance layer. Each player holds one of �=12 hands (131 non-conflictin<sub>g</sub> deals at the default seed); bettin<sub>g</sub> occurs on the turn and a<sub>g</sub>ain on the river, with fraction-of-pot sizing � = min(stack − committed, � · pot) and all-in handling (no river betting after all-in). Payofs use sunk-cost semantics: a turn fold loses the half-pot; a river fold additionally loses the caller’s <sub>comm</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ps; s</sub>h<sub>ow</sub>d<sub>own</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ers</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>a</sub>lf<sub>-po</sub>t <sub>p</sub>l<sub>us ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>wagers.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>au</sub>lt i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> h<sub>as</sub> 83<sub>,</sub>040 Inf<sub>ose</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>n 433<sub>,</sub>610 St<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es, a</sub>nd tr<sub>ee</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ldin<sub>g</sub> 8 f<sub>use</sub>d <sub>e</sub>x<sub>ecu</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s pe</sub>r <sub>pass.</sub>

Analytic anchors. Two closed-form checks pin absolute correctness of payofs, chance weighting, and the evaluator simultaneousl<sub>y</sub>. (i) River nuts anchor: if one <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er’s ran<sub>g</sub>e contains onl<sub>y</sub> the best <sub>p</sub>ossible hand, the e<sub>q</sub>uilibrium value in exact arithmetic is the half-<sub>p</sub>ot, +10; the im<sub>p</sub>lemented <sub>g</sub>ame’s chance discretization shifts this to an enumerable +10.000033, which the solver and a brute-force best-res<sub>p</sub>onse enumeration both repro<sup>d</sup>uce to <sup>1</sup>0<sup>−10</sup>. (<sup>ii</sup>) Qua<sup>d</sup>s anc<sup>h</sup>or: on t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>b</sup>oar<sup>d</sup> 7s 7d 7h Ks w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> <sup>h</sup>o<sup>l</sup>e car<sup>d</sup>s 7c 2d, <sup>f</sup>our-o<sup>f</sup>-a-<sup>ki</sup>n<sup>d</sup> wins all 44 rivers, forcin<sub>g</sub> root value +10; the solver conver<sub>g</sub>es to it with ex<sub>p</sub>loitabilit<sub>y</sub> below 10<sup>−3</sup> after 1,000 it<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

Best-response oracle. A recursive exact oracle, weighted by counterfactual reach and expanded deepest fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>, va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e gener</sub>i<sub>c eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>or on</sub> th<sub>ese games</sub> t<sub>o</sub> 10<sup>−9</sup><sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e orac</sub>l<sub>e</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf i<sub>s va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> b<sub>ru</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>f<sub>orce</sub> <sub>enumera</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>pure s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>eg</sub>i<sub>es on re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances.</sub>

External reconstruction of the river subgame. The river subgame can be reconstructed outside our codebase, <sub>prov</sub>idi<sub>ng an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ngs o</sub>f S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 7<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e express</sub> it <sub>as an</sub> O<sub>pen</sub>S<sub>p</sub>i<sub>e</sub>l universal\_poker <sup>ACPC i</sup>nstance (<sup>L</sup>anctot et a<sup>l</sup>., <sup>2020</sup>): <sup>fi</sup>xe<sup>d fi</sup>ve-car<sup>d b</sup>oar<sup>d</sup>, handReaches mar<sup>ki</sup>ng t<sup>h</sup>e �=100 candidate hands per player, and one betting round with two bet sizes. ## 10. Benchmark Protocol and Environment

Hardware and software. All measurements: one NVIDIA A100 80GB PCIe (dedicated, otherwise idle; driver 530.30) in a dual-socket host with two Intel Xeon Gold 6348 <sub>p</sub>rocessors (Ice Lake, 28 cores and 56 threads each at 2.6 GHz base, 112 lo<sub>g</sub>ical CPUs, two NUMA nodes), 256 GB of DDR4-3200 ECC memor<sub>y</sub> on ei<sub>g</sub>ht c<sup>h</sup>anne<sup>l</sup>s per soc<sup>k</sup>et, <sup>Li</sup>nux <sup>5</sup>.4 w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e ondemand <sup>f</sup>requency governor, <sup>GCC</sup> 9.4, <sup>P</sup>y<sup>T</sup>orc<sup>h 1</sup>.<sup>13</sup>.<sup>1</sup>+cu<sup>11</sup>7, <sup>CUDA</sup> 11.7, P<sub>y</sub>thon 3.9.16. Solve protocol. CFR<sup>+</sup> (regret matching<sup>+</sup>, linear averaging), simultaneous updates, float32, seed 0. The update-rule study of Table 6 departs from this protocol by design, sweeping four rules under both orders on the CPU at �=1,000 in float32. The certificate that scales: comparing on the value. The value is comparable across backends whatever each one runs, because a zero-sum tree has exactly one. Libratus endgame 4. The largest tree we put through all three backends is Libratus endgame 4 at full range: 36<sub>,</sub>098<sub>,</sub>785 nodes<sub>,</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>orted as a 2<sub>.</sub>42 GB file. Its scale is what the ex<sub>p</sub>ort mechanism bu<sub>y</sub>s and also what bounds it. GPU-CFR reads the end<sub>g</sub>ame in 165<sub>.</sub>889 s<sub>,</sub> com<sub>p</sub>iles it and constructs the solver in 124<sub>.</sub>751 s<sub>,</sub> and solves it on one A100 at a <sub>p</sub>eak of 14.4 GiB allocated (16.9 GiB reserved) in the benchmark <sub>p</sub>rocess, which also holds the float64 evaluation tree; the solver alone <sub>p</sub>eaks at 8.9 GiB (Table 9). Kim (2026) <sub>p</sub>arses the file in <sup>3</sup>99.<sup>3</sup>4<sup>1</sup> s an<sup>d</sup> so<sup>l</sup>ves <sup>i</sup>t on t<sup>h</sup>e same <sup>d</sup>ev<sup>i</sup>ce; <sup>Li</sup>te<sup>EFG’</sup>s FileEnv <sup>l</sup>oa<sup>d</sup>s <sup>i</sup>t at <sup>2</sup>4.4 <sup>GiB</sup> res<sup>id</sup>ent an<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>en <sup>i</sup>terates at 13.856 s, so 200 iterations is where all three meet. Table 7 (Section 7) re<sub>p</sub>orts that <sub>p</sub>oint. (16) allows, and the two GPU backends a<sub>g</sub>ree to 0<sub>.</sub>39 chi<sub>p</sub>s at 1<sub>,</sub>000 iterations.
